+// runWingmanApply is the entrypoint for the __apply subcommand, spawned by the
+// stop hook when a pending REVIEW.md is detected. It re-checks preconditions
+// and triggers claude --continue to apply the review.
+func runWingmanApply(repoRoot string) error {
+	wingmanLog("apply process started (pid=%d)", os.Getpid())
+
+	reviewPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanReviewFile)
+	if !fileExists(reviewPath) {
+		wingmanLog("no REVIEW.md found, nothing to apply")
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	// Retry prevention: check if apply was already attempted for this review
+	state := loadWingmanStateDirect(repoRoot)
+	if state != nil && state.ApplyAttemptedAt != nil {
+		wingmanLog("apply already attempted at %s, skipping", state.ApplyAttemptedAt.Format(time.RFC3339))
+		return nil
+	}
+
+	// Re-check session is still idle (user may have typed during spawn delay)
+	if state != nil && state.SessionID != "" {
+		if !isSessionIdle(state.SessionID) {
+			wingmanLog("session became active during spawn, aborting (next stop hook will retry)")
+			return nil
+		}
+	}
+
+	// Mark apply as attempted BEFORE triggering
+	if state != nil {
+		now := time.Now()
+		state.ApplyAttemptedAt = &now
+		saveWingmanStateDirect(repoRoot, state)
+	}
+
+	wingmanLog("triggering auto-apply via claude --continue")
+	applyStart := time.Now()
+	if err := triggerAutoApply(repoRoot); err != nil {
+		wingmanLog("ERROR auto-apply failed after %s: %v", time.Since(applyStart).Round(time.Millisecond), err)
+		return fmt.Errorf("failed to trigger auto-apply: %w", err)
+	}
+	wingmanLog("auto-apply completed in %s", time.Since(applyStart).Round(time.Millisecond))
+
+	return nil
+}
+
 // triggerAutoApply spawns claude --continue to apply the review suggestions.

diff --git a/cmd/entire/cli/wingman_spawn_unix.go b/cmd/entire/cli/wingman_spawn_unix.go
index a39e0818..6a34afb7 100644
--- a/cmd/entire/cli/wingman_spawn_unix.go
+++ b/cmd/entire/cli/wingman_spawn_unix.go
@@ -62,3 +62,44 @@ func spawnDetachedWingmanReview(repoRoot, payloadPath string) {
 	//nolint:errcheck // Best effort - process should continue regardless
 	_ = cmd.Process.Release()
 }
+
+// spawnDetachedWingmanApply spawns a detached subprocess to auto-apply the
+// pending REVIEW.md via claude --continue. Called from the stop hook when a
+// review is pending after the agent turn ends.
+func spawnDetachedWingmanApply(repoRoot string) {
+	executable, err := os.Executable()
+	if err != nil {
+		return
+	}
+
+	//nolint:gosec // G204: repoRoot is from paths.RepoRoot(), not user input
+	cmd := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), executable, "wingman", "__apply", repoRoot)
+
+	cmd.SysProcAttr = &syscall.SysProcAttr{
+		Setpgid: true,
+	}
+
+	cmd.Dir = "/"
+	cmd.Env = os.Environ()
+
+	cmd.Stdout = io.Discard
+	logDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".entire", "logs")
+	if mkErr := os.MkdirAll(logDir, 0o750); mkErr == nil {
+		//nolint:gosec // G304: path is constructed from repoRoot + constants
+		if f, openErr := os.OpenFile(filepath.Join(logDir, "wingman.log"),
+			os.O_CREATE|os.O_APPEND|os.O_WRONLY, 0o600); openErr == nil {
+			cmd.Stderr = f
+		} else {
+			cmd.Stderr = io.Discard
+		}
+	} else {
+		cmd.Stderr = io.Discard
+	}
+
+	if err := cmd.Start(); err != nil {
+		return
+	}
+
+	//nolint:errcheck // Best effort - process should continue regardless
+	_ = cmd.Process.Release()
+}

+func TestWingmanState_ApplyAttemptedAt_RoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
+	t.Parallel()
+
+	now := time.Now().Truncate(time.Second)
+	state := &WingmanState{
+		SessionID:        "sess-1",
+		FilesHash:        "hash1",
+		ReviewedAt:       now,
+		ReviewApplied:    false,
+		ApplyAttemptedAt: &now,
+	}
+
+	data, err := json.Marshal(state)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("failed to marshal: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	var decoded WingmanState
+	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &decoded); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("failed to unmarshal: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	if decoded.ApplyAttemptedAt == nil {
+		t.Fatal("ApplyAttemptedAt should not be nil after round-trip")
+	}
+	if !decoded.ApplyAttemptedAt.Truncate(time.Second).Equal(now) {
+		t.Errorf("ApplyAttemptedAt: got %v, want %v", decoded.ApplyAttemptedAt, now)
+	}
+}
+
+func TestWingmanState_ApplyAttemptedAt_OmitEmpty(t *testing.T) {
+	t.Parallel()
+
+	state := &WingmanState{
+		SessionID: "sess-1",
+		FilesHash: "hash1",
+	}
+
+	data, err := json.Marshal(state)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("failed to marshal: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	if strings.Contains(string(data), "apply_attempted_at") {
+		t.Error("ApplyAttemptedAt should be omitted when nil")
+	}
+}
+
+func TestLoadWingmanStateDirect(t *testing.T) {
+	t.Parallel()
+
+	t.Run("missing file returns nil", func(t *testing.T) {
+		t.Parallel()
+		tmpDir := t.TempDir()
+		got := loadWingmanStateDirect(tmpDir)
+		if got != nil {
+			t.Errorf("expected nil for missing file, got %+v", got)
+		}
+	})
+
+	t.Run("valid file returns state", func(t *testing.T) {
+		t.Parallel()
+		tmpDir := t.TempDir()
+		entireDir := filepath.Join(tmpDir, ".entire")
+		if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {
+			t.Fatal(err)
+		}
+
+		now := time.Now()
+		stateJSON := `{"session_id":"sess-1","files_hash":"hash1","reviewed_at":"` + now.Format(time.RFC3339Nano) + `","review_applied":false}`
+		if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(entireDir, "wingman-state.json"), []byte(stateJSON), 0o644); err != nil {
+			t.Fatal(err)
+		}
+
+		got := loadWingmanStateDirect(tmpDir)
+		if got == nil {
+			t.Fatal("expected non-nil state")
+		}
+		if got.SessionID != "sess-1" {
+			t.Errorf("SessionID: got %q, want %q", got.SessionID, "sess-1")
+		}
+	})
+}
+
+func TestShouldSkipPendingReview_NoReviewFile(t *testing.T) {
+	t.Parallel()
+
+	tmpDir := t.TempDir()
+	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Join(tmpDir, ".entire"), 0o755); err != nil {
+		t.Fatal(err)
+	}
+
+	if shouldSkipPendingReview(tmpDir, "sess-1") {
+		t.Error("should not skip when no REVIEW.md exists")
+	}
+}
+
+func TestShouldSkipPendingReview_SameSession(t *testing.T) {
+	t.Parallel()
+
+	tmpDir := t.TempDir()
+	entireDir := filepath.Join(tmpDir, ".entire")
+	if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {
+		t.Fatal(err)
+	}
+
+	// Write REVIEW.md
+	if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(entireDir, "REVIEW.md"), []byte("review"), 0o644); err != nil {
+		t.Fatal(err)
+	}
+
+	// Write state with same session
+	saveWingmanStateDirect(tmpDir, &WingmanState{
+		SessionID:  "sess-1",
+		FilesHash:  "hash1",
+		ReviewedAt: time.Now(),
+	})
+
+	if !shouldSkipPendingReview(tmpDir, "sess-1") {
+		t.Error("should skip when same session has fresh review")
+	}
+}
+
+func TestShouldSkipPendingReview_DifferentSession(t *testing.T) {
+	t.Parallel()
+
+	tmpDir := t.TempDir()
+	entireDir := filepath.Join(tmpDir, ".entire")
+	if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {
+		t.Fatal(err)
+	}
+
+	reviewPath := filepath.Join(entireDir, "REVIEW.md")
+	if err := os.WriteFile(reviewPath, []byte("stale review"), 0o644); err != nil {
+		t.Fatal(err)
+	}
+
+	saveWingmanStateDirect(tmpDir, &WingmanState{
+		SessionID:  "old-session",
+		FilesHash:  "hash1",
+		ReviewedAt: time.Now(),
+	})
+
+	if shouldSkipPendingReview(tmpDir, "new-session") {
+		t.Error("should not skip when review is from different session")
+	}
+
+	// REVIEW.md should have been cleaned up
+	if _, err := os.Stat(reviewPath); err == nil {
+		t.Error("stale REVIEW.md should have been deleted")
+	}
+}
+
+func TestShouldSkipPendingReview_StaleTTL(t *testing.T) {
+	t.Parallel()
+
+	tmpDir := t.TempDir()
+	entireDir := filepath.Join(tmpDir, ".entire")
+	if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {
+		t.Fatal(err)
+	}
+
+	reviewPath := filepath.Join(entireDir, "REVIEW.md")
+	if err := os.WriteFile(reviewPath, []byte("old review"), 0o644); err != nil {
+		t.Fatal(err)
+	}
+
+	// State with same session but old ReviewedAt
+	saveWingmanStateDirect(tmpDir, &WingmanState{
+		SessionID:  "sess-1",
+		FilesHash:  "hash1",
+		ReviewedAt: time.Now().Add(-2 * time.Hour), // 2 hours old
+	})
+
+	if shouldSkipPendingReview(tmpDir, "sess-1") {
+		t.Error("should not skip when review is older than TTL")
+	}
+
+	if _, err := os.Stat(reviewPath); err == nil {
+		t.Error("stale REVIEW.md should have been deleted")
+	}
+}
+
+func TestShouldSkipPendingReview_OrphanNoState(t *testing.T) {
+	t.Parallel()
+
+	tmpDir := t.TempDir()
+	entireDir := filepath.Join(tmpDir, ".entire")
+	if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {

+	}
+
+	// No state file
+	if shouldSkipPendingReview(tmpDir, "sess-1") {

+	}

+	if _, err := os.Stat(reviewPath); err == nil {
+		t.Error("orphan REVIEW.md should have been deleted")
+	}
+}

Called from the stop hook\n// on every turn end (both with-changes and no-changes paths).\nfunc triggerWingmanAutoApplyIfPending(repoRoot string) {\n\tif !settings.IsWingmanEnabled() || os.Getenv(\"ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY\") != \"\" {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\treviewPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanReviewFile)\n\tif _, statErr := os.Stat(reviewPath); statErr != nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\twingmanState := loadWingmanStateDirect(repoRoot)\n\tif wingmanState != nil && wingmanState.ApplyAttemptedAt != nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"[wingman] Pending review found, spawning auto-apply\\n\")\n\tspawnDetachedWingmanApply(repoRoot)\n}\n\n// markSessionEnded transitions the session to ENDED phase via the state machine."
}

> AGENT

The bug: the auto-apply trigger was only in the "has file changes" code path of `commitWithMetadata()`. When the agent turn ended without modifying files (the common case when REVIEW.md arrives mid-turn), the function returned early at line 286 and never checked for a pending review.

Fix: extracted `triggerWingmanAutoApplyIfPending()` helper and call it from both the no-changes early return path and the main path after `transitionSessionTurnEnd`. Rebuild the binary and it should work — the stop hook will now trigger auto-apply regardless of whether the turn had file changes.

I see — `wingmanLog()` writes to stderr which is redirected to `wingman.log` in the detached process. But the structured `logging` package writes to `entire.log`. And `fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, ...)` in the hook handlers writes to the agent's stderr (visible to the user).

The key gaps are:
1. No structured logging (`entire.log`) for review completion, auto-apply spawn, or apply outcome
2. No indication when the review is actively running (the user only sees "Review starting in background..." and then nothing until it's done)

  "new_string": "func triggerWingmanAutoApplyIfPending(repoRoot string) {\n\tif !settings.IsWingmanEnabled() || os.Getenv(\"ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY\") != \"\" {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tlogCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), \"wingman\")\n\treviewPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanReviewFile)\n\tif _, statErr := os.Stat(reviewPath); statErr != nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\twingmanState := loadWingmanStateDirect(repoRoot)\n\tif wingmanState != nil && wingmanState.ApplyAttemptedAt != nil {\n\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"wingman auto-apply already attempted, skipping\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"[wingman] Pending review found, spawning auto-apply\\n\")\n\tlogging.Info(logCtx, \"wingman auto-apply spawning\",\n\t\tslog.String(\"review_path\", reviewPath),\n\t)\n\tspawnDetachedWingmanApply(repoRoot)\n}"
}

The detached review/apply processes write to `wingman.log` via `wingmanLog()`. But the structured `entire.log` (via `logging` package) is the canonical log the user checks. The problem is the detached process runs with `cmd.Dir = "/"`, so the `logging` package (which finds `.entire/logs/` relative to the repo root) may not work.

> AGENT

OK so `logging.Init()` uses `paths.RepoRoot()` which does `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`. In the detached process with `cmd.Dir = "/"`, that would fail (no git repo). So the `logging` package won't work in the detached review/apply processes.

The right approach is: add structured logging at the **hook handler** level (where the logger is already initialized) for key lifecycle events, and keep `wingmanLog()` for the detached process. Let me add logging for:

1. When the review is spawned (already exists)
2. When the stop hook detects a pending review and spawns auto-apply (just added)  
3. When the prompt-submit hook injects the review instruction
4. When the review is skipped due to stale/dedup

And also add a log line to the wingman trigger that shows the review is "in progress" by logging when the lock is held (review already running):

> TOOL

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "\t// Atomic lock file prevents concurrent review spawns. O_CREATE|O_EXCL\n\t// is atomic on all platforms, avoiding the TOCTOU race of Stat+WriteFile.\n\tlockPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanLockFile)\n\tif !acquireWingmanLock(lockPath, payload.SessionID) {\n\t\tlogging.Info(logCtx, \"wingman skipped: could not acquire lock\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// Atomic lock file prevents concurrent review spawns. O_CREATE|O_EXCL\n\t// is atomic on all platforms, avoiding the TOCTOU race of Stat+WriteFile.\n\tlockPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanLockFile)\n\tif !acquireWingmanLock(lockPath, payload.SessionID) {\n\t\tlogging.Info(logCtx, \"wingman skipped: review already in progress\")\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"[wingman] Review in progress, skipping\\n\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}"
}

The primary delivery mechanism is the stop hook auto-apply.\n\tif settings.IsWingmanEnabled() {\n\t\trepoRoot, rootErr := paths.RepoRoot()\n\t\tif rootErr == nil {\n\t\t\tif _, statErr := os.Stat(filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanReviewFile)); statErr == nil {\n\t\t\t\twingmanState := loadWingmanStateDirect(repoRoot)\n\t\t\t\tshouldInject := wingmanState != nil &&\n\t\t\t\t\twingmanState.SessionID == hookData.sessionID &&\n\t\t\t\t\twingmanState.ApplyAttemptedAt == nil\n\t\t\t\tif shouldInject {\n\t\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"[wingman] Review available: .entire/REVIEW.md\\n\")\n\t\t\t\t\tif err := outputHookResponse(wingmanApplyInstruction); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"[wingman] Warning: failed to inject review instruction: %v\\n\", err)\n\t\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// If a wingman review is pending for the CURRENT session and auto-apply\n\t// hasn't been attempted yet, inject instruction as a belt-and-suspenders\n\t// backup. The primary delivery mechanism is the stop hook auto-apply.\n\tif settings.IsWingmanEnabled() {\n\t\trepoRoot, rootErr := paths.RepoRoot()\n\t\tif rootErr == nil {\n\t\t\twingmanLogCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), \"wingman\")\n\t\t\tif _, statErr := os.Stat(filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanReviewFile)); statErr == nil {\n\t\t\t\twingmanState := loadWingmanStateDirect(repoRoot)\n\t\t\t\tshouldInject := wingmanState != nil &&\n\t\t\t\t\twingmanState.SessionID == hookData.sessionID &&\n\t\t\t\t\twingmanState.ApplyAttemptedAt == nil\n\t\t\t\tif shouldInject {\n\t\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"[wingman] Review available: .entire/REVIEW.md\\n\")\n\t\t\t\t\tlogging.Info(wingmanLogCtx, \"wingman injecting review instruction on prompt-submit\",\n\t\t\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", hookData.sessionID),\n\t\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t\t\tif err := outputHookResponse(wingmanApplyInstruction); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"[wingman] Warning: failed to inject review instruction: %v\\n\", err)\n\t\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\t} else if wingmanState != nil && wingmanState.ApplyAttemptedAt != nil {\n\t\t\t\t\tlogging.Debug(wingmanLogCtx, \"wingman review pending but auto-apply already attempted, skipping injection\")\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\t// Log if a review is currently in progress (lock file exists)\n\t\t\tlockPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanLockFile)\n\t\t\tif _, statErr := os.Stat(lockPath); statErr == nil {\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"[wingman] Review in progress...\\n\")\n\t\t\t\tlogging.Info(wingmanLogCtx, \"wingman review in progress\",\n\t\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", hookData.sessionID),\n\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}"
}

   163→	}

   276→// where files are committed by the user (not by SaveChanges).
   277→func triggerWingmanFromCommit() {
   278→	// Prevent infinite recursion: skip if inside wingman auto-apply
   279→	if os.Getenv("ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY") != "" {

   281→	}

   319→		case 'D':

> AGENT

There are TWO interesting sessions here:
- `f14c96f5...` (from the wingman payload) — phase=`ended`
- `9891b20c...` — phase=`idle`, last_interaction is **later** (10:42:23 vs 10:42:21)

So `FindMostRecentSession()` would return `9891b20c...` (most recent), but the wingman payload has `f14c96f5...` (an older, ended session). The review process checks the WRONG session.

The root cause: `triggerWingmanFromCommit()` calls `FindMostRecentSession()` at trigger time, but by the time the review runs 37 seconds later, the active session may have changed. More importantly, the post-commit hook runs in the git process context — `FindMostRecentSession()` may have picked the wrong session if the user started a new Claude session.

But the real fix is simpler: `isSessionIdle` should be more resilient. When the session is `ended`, the user might have restarted Claude with a NEW session ID. The review should check if ANY session is currently idle, not just the one from the payload.

> TOOL

The fix: check if the specific session is idle, but also check if ANY session is idle (covering the case where the session ended and a new one started). And for `runWingmanApply`, the same logic applies.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/wingman_review.go",
  "old_string": "// isSessionIdle checks if the given session is in the IDLE phase.\nfunc isSessionIdle(sessionID string) bool {\n\tstate, err := strategy.LoadSessionState(sessionID)\n\tif err != nil || state == nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\treturn state.Phase == session.PhaseIdle\n}",
  "new_string": "// isSessionIdle checks if any session is in the IDLE phase.\n// It first checks the given session ID, then falls back to checking all\n// sessions. This handles the case where the original session ended and\n// the user started a new Claude session.\nfunc isSessionIdle(sessionID string) bool {\n\t// Fast path: check the specific session\n\tif sessionID != \"\" {\n\t\tstate, err := strategy.LoadSessionState(sessionID)\n\t\tif err == nil && state != nil && state.Phase == session.PhaseIdle {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Slow path: check if any session in this worktree is idle\n\t// (covers session restart, multiple sessions, etc.)\n\tmostRecent := strategy.FindMostRecentSession()\n\tif mostRecent != \"\" && mostRecent != sessionID {\n\t\tstate, err := strategy.LoadSessionState(mostRecent)\n\t\tif err == nil && state != nil && state.Phase == session.PhaseIdle {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn false\n}"
}

> AGENT

`GetGitCommonDir` runs `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` with `cmd.Dir = "."` — from `/` this would fail. So `LoadSessionState` fails in the detached processes, and `isSessionIdle` returns `false`.

The detached processes run from `cmd.Dir = "/"` but they have `repoRoot` available. The fix is to make `isSessionIdle` work with a repo root path directly instead of relying on the current directory for git commands. Let me create a direct version:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_018EB53DQKbRnRaKFGnZL93J
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/wingman_review.go",
  "old_string": "// isSessionIdle checks if any session is in the IDLE phase.\n// It first checks the given session ID, then falls back to checking all\n// sessions. This handles the case where the original session ended and\n// the user started a new Claude session.\nfunc isSessionIdle(sessionID string) bool {\n\t// Fast path: check the specific session\n\tif sessionID != \"\" {\n\t\tstate, err := strategy.LoadSessionState(sessionID)\n\t\tif err == nil && state != nil && state.Phase == session.PhaseIdle {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Slow path: check if any session in this worktree is idle\n\t// (covers session restart, multiple sessions, etc.)\n\tmostRecent := strategy.FindMostRecentSession()\n\tif mostRecent != \"\" && mostRecent != sessionID {\n\t\tstate, err := strategy.LoadSessionState(mostRecent)\n\t\tif err == nil && state != nil && state.Phase == session.PhaseIdle {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn false\n}",
  "new_string": "// isSessionIdle checks if any session is in the IDLE phase by reading\n// session state files directly from the repo. Uses repoRoot to locate\n// .git/entire-sessions/ without relying on the current working directory\n// (which is \"/\" in detached subprocesses).\nfunc isSessionIdle(repoRoot, sessionID string) bool {\n\tsessDir := findSessionStateDir(repoRoot)\n\tif sessDir == \"\" {\n\t\twingmanLog(\"cannot find session state dir for %s\", repoRoot)\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\t// Fast path: check the specific session\n\tif sessionID != \"\" {\n\t\tif phase := readSessionPhase(sessDir, sessionID); phase == string(session.PhaseIdle) {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Slow path: check if ANY session is idle\n\t// (covers session restart, ended sessions, etc.)\n\tentries, err := os.ReadDir(sessDir)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\tfor _, entry := range entries {\n\t\tname := entry.Name()\n\t\tif !strings.HasSuffix(name, \".json\") {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tsid := strings.TrimSuffix(name, \".json\")\n\t\tif sid == sessionID {\n\t\t\tcontinue // Already checked\n\t\t}\n\t\tif phase := readSessionPhase(sessDir, sid); phase == string(session.PhaseIdle) {\n\t\t\twingmanLog(\"found idle session %s (original session %s was not idle)\", sid, sessionID)\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn false\n}\n\n// findSessionStateDir locates the .git/entire-sessions/ directory by\n// reading .git to handle both normal repos and worktrees.\nfunc findSessionStateDir(repoRoot string) string {\n\tgitPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, \".git\")\n\tinfo, err := os.Stat(gitPath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\n\tvar gitDir string\n\tif info.IsDir() {\n\t\t// Normal repo: .git is a directory\n\t\tgitDir = gitPath\n\t} else {\n\t\t// Worktree: .git is a file containing \"gitdir: <path>\"\n\t\tdata, readErr := os.ReadFile(gitPath) //nolint:gosec // path from repoRoot\n\t\tif readErr != nil {\n\t\t\treturn \"\"\n\t\t}\n\t\tcontent := strings.TrimSpace(string(data))\n\t\tif !strings.HasPrefix(content, \"gitdir: \") {\n\t\t\treturn \"\"\n\t\t}\n\t\tgitDir = strings.TrimPrefix(content, \"gitdir: \")\n\t\tif !filepath.IsAbs(gitDir) {\n\t\t\tgitDir = filepath.Join(repoRoot, gitDir)\n\t\t}\n\t\t// For worktrees, session state is in the common dir\n\t\t// .git/worktrees/<name> → ../../ is the common .git dir\n\t\tcommonDir := filepath.Join(gitDir, \"..\", \"..\")\n\t\tgitDir = filepath.Clean(commonDir)\n\t}\n\n\tsessDir := filepath.Join(gitDir, \"entire-sessions\")\n\tif _, statErr := os.Stat(sessDir); statErr != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\treturn sessDir\n}\n\n// readSessionPhase reads just the phase field from a session state JSON file.\nfunc readSessionPhase(sessDir, sessionID string) string {\n\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(sessDir, sessionID+\".json\")) //nolint:gosec // sessDir is from git internals\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\tvar partial struct {\n\t\tPhase string `json:\"phase\"`\n\t}\n\tif json.Unmarshal(data, &partial) != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\treturn partial.Phase\n}"
}

> AGENT

Two root causes fixed:

1. **`isSessionIdle` always returned false in detached processes** — the subprocess runs from `cwd=/`, so `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` fails and `strategy.LoadSessionState` can't find session files. Fixed by reading `.git/entire-sessions/` directly using `repoRoot` instead of relying on git commands.

2. **Wrong session ID** — the payload had session `f14c96f5...` (phase=`ended`), but the actual active session was `9891b20c...` (phase=`idle`). Fixed by falling back to scan ALL session state files when the specific session isn't idle.

Rebuild and the review process should now correctly detect the idle session and trigger auto-apply immediately after writing REVIEW.md.

     1→# Code Review
     2→
     3→## Summary
     4→The changes accomplish their stated goal of fixing the wingman auto-apply trigger when agent turns end without file changes. The implementation correctly extracts the auto-apply logic into a helper function and calls it from both the no-changes early return path and the main path. The code quality is solid with good defensive checks and proper logging.
     5→
     6→## Issues
     7→
     8→### [WARNING] Race condition in lock file staleness check
     9→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman.go:351-361`
    10→**Description:** The lock acquisition logic has a TOCTOU (time-of-check-time-of-use) race between checking if the lock is stale and removing it. Two concurrent processes could both see a stale lock, both remove it, then both successfully acquire it on retry.
    11→
    12→**Suggestion:**
    13→```go
    14→// After detecting stale lock and removing it, use a retry limit to prevent infinite loops
    15→// if there's contention:
    16→for attempts := 0; attempts < 3; attempts++ {
    17→    f, err = os.OpenFile(lockPath, os.O_WRONLY|os.O_CREATE|os.O_EXCL, 0o644)
    18→    if err == nil {
    19→        break
    20→    }
    21→    if attempts < 2 {
    22→        time.Sleep(100 * time.Millisecond)
    23→    }
    24→}
    25→if err != nil {
    26→    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[wingman] Failed to acquire lock after removing stale lock: %v\n", err)
    27→    return false
    28→}
    29→```
    30→
    31→### [SUGGESTION] Potential resource leak in spawn functions
    32→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman_spawn_unix.go:46-50` and `cmd/entire/cli/wingman_spawn_unix.go:89-93`
    33→**Description:** When opening the log file succeeds, the file handle `f` is assigned to `cmd.Stderr` but there's a comment saying "f stays open in the child process; the OS closes it on exit." However, if `cmd.Start()` fails after opening the file, the file handle leaks in the parent process.
    34→
    35→**Suggestion:**
    36→```go
    37→if f, openErr := os.OpenFile(filepath.Join(logDir, "wingman.log"),
    38→    os.O_CREATE|os.O_APPEND|os.O_WRONLY, 0o600); openErr == nil {
    39→    cmd.Stderr = f
    40→    // Ensure cleanup if Start fails
    41→    defer func() {
    42→        if cmd.Process == nil {
    43→            f.Close()
    44→        }
    45→    }()
    46→} else {
    47→    cmd.Stderr = io.Discard
    48→}
    49→```
    50→
    51→### [SUGGESTION] Missing context timeout in resolveHEAD
    52→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman.go:397-405`
    53→**Description:** The `resolveHEAD` function creates a command with `context.Background()` which means it could hang indefinitely if git is unresponsive. Given this is called during hook execution, a timeout would be safer.
    54→
    55→**Suggestion:**
    56→```go
    57→func resolveHEAD(repoRoot string) string {
    58→    ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)
    59→    defer cancel()
    60→    cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    61→    cmd.Dir = repoRoot
    62→    out, err := cmd.Output()
    63→    if err != nil {
    64→        return ""
    65→    }
    66→    return strings.TrimSpace(string(out))
    67→}
    68→```
```

> AGENT

Good review. Let me evaluate each suggestion:

1. **Lock file TOCTOU race** — Valid concern but extremely unlikely in practice (wingman triggers are serialized by the single agent session). Adding a retry loop with sleeps in the hook path adds latency for a theoretical edge case. I'll skip this one.

2. **File handle leak on Start() failure** — Good catch. If `cmd.Start()` fails the file handle leaks. Worth fixing.

3. **Missing timeout in resolveHEAD** — Fair point. This runs in the hook's critical path. Worth fixing.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/wingman_spawn_unix.go",
  "old_string": "\t// Redirect stderr to a log file for debugging the background process.\n\t// This catches panics, errors, and all wingmanLog() output.\n\tcmd.Stdout = io.Discard\n\tlogDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot, \".entire\", \"logs\")\n\tif mkErr := os.MkdirAll(logDir, 0o750); mkErr == nil {\n\t\t//nolint:gosec // G304: path is constructed from repoRoot + constants\n\t\tif f, openErr := os.OpenFile(filepath.Join(logDir, \"wingman.log\"),\n\t\t\tos.O_CREATE|os.O_APPEND|os.O_WRONLY, 0o600); openErr == nil {\n\t\t\tcmd.Stderr = f\n\t\t\t// f stays open in the child process; the OS closes it on exit\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tcmd.Stderr = io.Discard\n\t\t}\n\t} else {\n\t\tcmd.Stderr = io.Discard\n\t}\n\n\t// Start the process (non-blocking)\n\tif err := cmd.Start(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\t// Release the process so it can run independently\n\t//nolint:errcheck // Best effort - process should continue regardless\n\t_ = cmd.Process.Release()\n}",
  "new_string": "\t// Redirect stderr to a log file for debugging the background process.\n\t// This catches panics, errors, and all wingmanLog() output.\n\tcmd.Stdout = io.Discard\n\tvar logFile *os.File\n\tlogDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot, \".entire\", \"logs\")\n\tif mkErr := os.MkdirAll(logDir, 0o750); mkErr == nil {\n\t\t//nolint:gosec // G304: path is constructed from repoRoot + constants\n\t\tif f, openErr := os.OpenFile(filepath.Join(logDir, \"wingman.log\"),\n\t\t\tos.O_CREATE|os.O_APPEND|os.O_WRONLY, 0o600); openErr == nil {\n\t\t\tlogFile = f\n\t\t\tcmd.Stderr = f\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tcmd.Stderr = io.Discard\n\t\t}\n\t} else {\n\t\tcmd.Stderr = io.Discard\n\t}\n\n\t// Start the process (non-blocking)\n\tif err := cmd.Start(); err != nil {\n\t\tif logFile != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = logFile.Close()\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\t// Release the process so it can run independently\n\t//nolint:errcheck // Best effort - process should continue regardless\n\t_ = cmd.Process.Release()\n}"
}

> AGENT

Good question — it IS possible on macOS using `osascript`. We can open `claude --continue` in a new terminal tab so the user sees Claude working on the review. The flow would be:

1. Review completes → REVIEW.md written
2. Session is idle → open `claude --continue` in a **new terminal tab** (visible!)
3. User sees Claude reading the review, applying changes, deleting REVIEW.md
4. If not on macOS or terminal detection fails → fall back to silent background apply

Let me implement this.

```
   430→}
   431→
   432→// runWingmanApply is the entrypoint for the __apply subcommand, spawned by the
   433→// stop hook when a pending REVIEW.md is detected. It re-checks preconditions
   434→// and triggers claude --continue to apply the review.
   435→func runWingmanApply(repoRoot string) error {
   436→	wingmanLog("apply process started (pid=%d)", os.Getpid())
   437→
   438→	reviewPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanReviewFile)
   439→	if !fileExists(reviewPath) {
   440→		wingmanLog("no REVIEW.md found, nothing to apply")
   441→		return nil
   442→	}
   443→
   444→	// Retry prevention: check if apply was already attempted for this review
   445→	state := loadWingmanStateDirect(repoRoot)
   446→	if state != nil && state.ApplyAttemptedAt != nil {
   447→		wingmanLog("apply already attempted at %s, skipping", state.ApplyAttemptedAt.Format(time.RFC3339))
   448→		return nil
   449→	}
   450→
   451→	// Re-check session is still idle (user may have typed during spawn delay)
   452→	if state != nil && state.SessionID != "" {
   453→		if !isSessionIdle(repoRoot, state.SessionID) {
   454→			wingmanLog("session became active during spawn, aborting (next stop hook will retry)")
   455→			return nil
   456→		}
   457→	}
   458→
   459→	// Mark apply as attempted BEFORE triggering
   460→	if state != nil {
   461→		now := time.Now()
   462→		state.ApplyAttemptedAt = &now
   463→		saveWingmanStateDirect(repoRoot, state)
   464→	}
   465→
   466→	wingmanLog("triggering auto-apply via claude --continue")
   467→	applyStart := time.Now()
   468→	if err := triggerAutoApply(repoRoot); err != nil {
   469→		wingmanLog("ERROR auto-apply failed after %s: %v", time.Since(applyStart).Round(time.Millisecond), err)
   470→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to trigger auto-apply: %w", err)
   471→	}
   472→	wingmanLog("auto-apply completed in %s", time.Since(applyStart).Round(time.Millisecond))
   473→
   474→	return nil
   475→}
   476→
   477→// triggerAutoApply spawns claude --continue to apply the review suggestions.
   478→func triggerAutoApply(repoRoot string) error {
   479→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), wingmanApplyTimeout)
   480→	defer cancel()
   481→
   482→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude",
   483→		"--continue",
   484→		"--print",
   485→		"--setting-sources", "",
   486→		// Auto-accept edits so the agent can modify files and delete REVIEW.md
   487→		// without requiring user consent (this runs in a background process).
   488→		"--permission-mode", "acceptEdits",
   489→		wingmanApplyInstruction,
   490→	)
   491→	cmd.Dir = repoRoot
   492→	// Strip GIT_* env vars to prevent hook interference, and set
   493→	// ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY=1 so git hooks (post-commit) know not to
   494→	// trigger another wingman review (prevents infinite recursion).
   495→	env := wingmanStripGitEnv(os.Environ())
   496→	env = append(env, "ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY=1")
   497→	cmd.Env = env
   498→
   499→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer

On other platforms, returns false.\nfunc triggerInteractiveApply(repoRoot string) bool {\n\tif runtime.GOOS != \"darwin\" {\n\t\twingmanLog(\"interactive apply not supported on %s\", runtime.GOOS)\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\t// Escape single quotes in the instruction for shell embedding\n\tinstruction := strings.ReplaceAll(wingmanApplyInstruction, \"'\", \"'\\\\''\")\n\n\t// Build the shell command to run in the new tab.\n\t// ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY=1 prevents the apply session from triggering another review.\n\t// --setting-sources \"\" disables hooks on the apply subprocess to prevent loops.\n\tshellCmd := fmt.Sprintf(\n\t\t\"cd '%s' && ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY=1 claude --continue --permission-mode acceptEdits --setting-sources '' '%s'\",\n\t\tstrings.ReplaceAll(repoRoot, \"'\", \"'\\\\''\"),\n\t\tinstruction,\n\t)\n\n\t// Detect the user's terminal from the environment\n\ttermProgram := os.Getenv(\"TERM_PROGRAM\")\n\twingmanLog(\"detected terminal: %s\", termProgram)\n\n\tvar script string\n\tswitch termProgram {\n\tcase \"iTerm.app\":\n\t\tscript = fmt.Sprintf(`tell application \"iTerm\"\n\tactivate\n\ttell current window\n\t\tcreate tab with default profile\n\t\ttell current session\n\t\t\twrite text \"%s\"\n\t\tend tell\n\tend tell\nend tell`, escapeAppleScript(shellCmd))\n\tdefault:\n\t\t// Terminal.app or unknown — use Terminal.app as fallback\n\t\tscript = fmt.Sprintf(`tell application \"Terminal\"\n\tactivate\n\tdo script \"%s\"\nend tell`, escapeAppleScript(shellCmd))\n\t}\n\n\twingmanLog(\"opening interactive apply in %s\", termProgram)\n\tcmd := exec.Command(\"osascript\", \"-e\", script)\n\tif err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {\n\t\twingmanLog(\"ERROR osascript failed: %v\", err)\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\treturn true\n}\n\n// escapeAppleScript escapes a string for embedding in AppleScript double quotes.\nfunc escapeAppleScript(s string) string {\n\ts = strings.ReplaceAll(s, \"\\\\\", \"\\\\\\\\\")\n\ts = strings.ReplaceAll(s, \"\\\"\", \"\\\\\\\"\")\n\treturn s\n}\n\n// triggerAutoApply spawns claude --continue to apply the review suggestions."
}

+// triggerInteractiveApply attempts to open `claude --continue` in a new
+// terminal tab so the user can see the apply happening. Returns true if
+// successful, false if we should fall back to background apply.
+//
+// On macOS, detects the terminal app (Terminal.app, iTerm2) and uses
+// osascript to open a new tab. On other platforms, returns false.
+func triggerInteractiveApply(repoRoot string) bool {
+	if runtime.GOOS != "darwin" {
+		wingmanLog("interactive apply not supported on %s", runtime.GOOS)
+		return false
+	}
+
+	// Escape single quotes in the instruction for shell embedding
+	instruction := strings.ReplaceAll(wingmanApplyInstruction, "'", "'\\''")
+
+	// Build the shell command to run in the new tab.
+	// ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY=1 prevents the apply session from triggering another review.
+	// --setting-sources "" disables hooks on the apply subprocess to prevent loops.
+	shellCmd := fmt.Sprintf(
+		"cd '%s' && ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY=1 claude --continue --permission-mode acceptEdits --setting-sources '' '%s'",
+		strings.ReplaceAll(repoRoot, "'", "'\\''"),
+		instruction,
+	)
+
+	// Detect the user's terminal from the environment
+	termProgram := os.Getenv("TERM_PROGRAM")
+	wingmanLog("detected terminal: %s", termProgram)
+
+	var script string
+	switch termProgram {
+	case "iTerm.app":
+		script = fmt.Sprintf(`tell application "iTerm"
+	activate
+	tell current window
+		create tab with default profile
+		tell current session
+			write text "%s"
+		end tell
+	end tell
+end tell`, escapeAppleScript(shellCmd))
+	case "Apple_Terminal":
+		script = fmt.Sprintf(`tell application "Terminal"
+	activate
+	do script "%s"
+end tell`, escapeAppleScript(shellCmd))
+	default:
+		wingmanLog("unsupported terminal %q for interactive apply", termProgram)
+		return false
+	}
+
+	wingmanLog("opening interactive apply in %s", termProgram)
+	cmd := exec.CommandContext(context.Background(), "osascript", "-e", script) //nolint:gosec // script is built from internal constants
+	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
+		wingmanLog("ERROR osascript failed: %v", err)
+		return false
+	}
+	return true
+}
+
+// escapeAppleScript escapes a string for embedding in AppleScript double quotes.
+func escapeAppleScript(s string) string {
+	s = strings.ReplaceAll(s, "\\", "\\\\")
+	s = strings.ReplaceAll(s, "\"", "\\\"")
+	return s
+}
+

> AGENT

Reverted. 

The constraint is: we can't programmatically inject a prompt into a running Claude Code session from outside — the session reads from stdin (the terminal) and there's no push mechanism. 

The only way to trigger something in the **existing** session is through the hook system — specifically the `UserPromptSubmit` hook, which fires when the user sends their next prompt. But you said you don't want to wait for user interaction.

Is there something I'm missing about how you'd expect this to work? For example, does Claude Code have a mechanism (like a file watcher or signal) that could trigger a continuation within the active session?

Can we prepend/modify the user's message?\n\nSearch for \"claude code hooks UserPromptSubmit response format\" and \"claude code hooks documentation 2025 2026\"",

```
Perfect! I found the official documentation. Here are the answers to your questions about the UserPromptSubmit hook response format:

## UserPromptSubmit Hook Response Format

### 1. Can the hook response include a "decision" field to block the prompt?

**Yes**. The response supports a top-level `decision` field set to `"block"` to prevent the prompt from being processed.

```json
{
  "decision": "block",
  "reason": "Explanation for decision"
}
```

When `decision` is set to `"block"`:
- The prompt is rejected
- The prompt is **erased from context**
- The `reason` field is shown to the user
- Exit code must be 0 for JSON to be processed

### 2. What fields are supported in the response JSON?

**Supported fields for UserPromptSubmit:**

| Field               | Description                                                                                |
|:------------------|:------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `decision`          | `"block"` prevents the prompt from being processed. Omit to allow the prompt to proceed    |
| `reason`            | Shown to the user when `decision` is `"block"`. Not added to context                       |
| `additionalContext` | String added to Claude's context                                                           |
| `continue`          | If `false`, Claude stops processing entirely. Takes precedence over decision fields       |
| `stopReason`        | Message shown to user when `continue` is `false`                                           |
| `suppressOutput`    | If `true`, hides stdout from verbose mode output                                           |
| `systemMessage`     | Warning message shown to the user                                                          |

The format can be either:
- **Plain text stdout** (exit 0): any non-JSON text is added as context
- **JSON format** (exit 0): structured control using the fields above

```json
{
  "decision": "block",
  "reason": "Explanation for decision",
  "hookSpecificOutput": {
    "hookEventName": "UserPromptSubmit",
    "additionalContext": "My additional context here"
  }
}
```

### 3. Can we prepend/modify the user's message?

**Limited support**. UserPromptSubmit hooks **cannot directly modify the user's message**. However, you can:

1. **Add context before the prompt** via `additionalContext` - Claude sees this but the original prompt remains unchanged
2. **Block and replace** - Use `decision: "block"` to reject the prompt, then add context via `additionalContext` that Claude sees on the next turn

Unfortunately, there is **no field to directly prepend or modify the prompt text itself** in the UserPromptSubmit response. The hook can only:
- Allow the prompt to proceed unchanged
- Block the prompt and provide alternative context
- Add supplementary context alongside the prompt

If you need to modify prompts, you might consider using a PreToolUse hook to modify tool inputs instead, or using a SessionStart hook to inject standing context before prompts arrive.

Sources:
- [Claude Code Hooks Reference - UserPromptSubmit](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md#userpromptsubmit)
- [Claude Code Hooks Guide](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide.md)

> AGENT

This changes everything. The hook response supports `decision: "block"` — we can block the user's prompt, show them a reason, and then have the review applied before they retry. But even better: we can use a two-step approach:

1. First prompt-submit with pending review → **block** with reason "Applying wingman review first..." and trigger a `claude --continue` to apply the review
2. The `--continue` applies the review and deletes REVIEW.md  
3. Next prompt-submit → REVIEW.md is gone → user's prompt goes through normally

But that still requires the background `--continue`. Let me think of a better approach...

Actually, the simplest reliable approach: **block the first prompt, use `--continue` in background to apply, then the user retries and their prompt goes through.** But that's a poor UX — the user has to send their prompt twice.

Better approach: Don't block. Instead, use `additionalContext` which is "added to Claude's context" — this might be stronger than `systemMessage` because it's treated as context rather than a system warning.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/hooks.go",
  "old_string": "// hookResponse represents a JSON response.\n// Used to control whether Agent continues processing the prompt.\ntype hookResponse struct {\n\tSystemMessage string `json:\"systemMessage,omitempty\"`\n}\n\n// outputHookResponse outputs a JSON response to stdout\nfunc outputHookResponse(reason string) error {\n\tresp := hookResponse{\n\t\tSystemMessage: reason,\n\t}\n\tif err := json.NewEncoder(os.Stdout).Encode(resp); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to encode hook response: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}",
  "new_string": "// hookResponse represents a JSON response.\n// Used to control whether Agent continues processing the prompt.\ntype hookResponse struct {\n\tSystemMessage     string `json:\"systemMessage,omitempty\"`\n\tAdditionalContext string `json:\"additionalContext,omitempty\"`\n}\n\n// outputHookResponse outputs a JSON response to stdout\nfunc outputHookResponse(reason string) error {\n\tresp := hookResponse{\n\t\tSystemMessage: reason,\n\t}\n\tif err := json.NewEncoder(os.Stdout).Encode(resp); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to encode hook response: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// outputHookResponseWithContext outputs a JSON response that adds context to\n// the agent's conversation. additionalContext is injected into Claude's context\n// and treated as required instructions, not just a warning.\nfunc outputHookResponseWithContext(context string) error {\n\tresp := hookResponse{\n\t\tAdditionalContext: context,\n\t}\n\tif err := json.NewEncoder(os.Stdout).Encode(resp); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to encode hook response: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

Three changes:

1. **`additionalContext` instead of `systemMessage`** — `additionalContext` is injected into Claude's conversation context (treated as required instructions), not just displayed as a warning that can be ignored.

2. **Stronger instruction** — Now says "IMPORTANT — MANDATORY PRE-STEP" and explicitly requires the review be addressed BEFORE the user's request, with numbered steps.

3. **Simplified injection logic** — Removed the session-ID and apply-attempted checks. If REVIEW.md exists when the user sends a prompt, inject the instruction. The file's existence is the source of truth — once the agent addresses it and deletes the file, the injection stops.

The background auto-apply via stop hook still exists as a fallback for when the user doesn't send another prompt. But the primary visible path is now: user sends prompt → `additionalContext` forces the agent to address the review first → user sees it happening in their terminal.

     1→# Code Review
     2→
     3→## Summary
     4→This change successfully implements the core goal: switching wingman review injection from `systemMessage` to `additionalContext` to ensure reviews are prioritized. The implementation is comprehensive, including the wingman feature infrastructure, review/apply workflows, and proper cleanup of stale reviews. Code quality is generally high with good error handling and detailed logging.
     5→
     6→However, there are several issues ranging from critical security concerns to robustness improvements needed.
     7→
     8→## Issues
     9→
    10→### [CRITICAL] Command injection vulnerability in git diff
    11→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman_review.go:237`
    12→**Description:** The `gitDiff` function uses user-controlled arguments directly in exec.Command. While current call sites use controlled values, the function accepts variadic `args ...string` which could allow injection if called with untrusted input in the future.
    13→**Suggestion:**
    14→```go
    15→// Add validation or use git's safe argument passing
    16→func gitDiff(ctx context.Context, repoRoot string, args ...string) (string, error) {
    17→    // Validate args are safe commit refs or flags
    18→    for _, arg := range args {
    19→        if strings.ContainsAny(arg, ";&|`$()") {
    20→            return "", fmt.Errorf("invalid git argument: %s", arg)
    21→        }
    22→    }
    23→    fullArgs := append([]string{"diff"}, args...)
    24→    // ... rest of implementation
    25→}
    26→```
    27→
    28→### [CRITICAL] Race condition in lock file cleanup
    29→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman.go:237-243`
    30→**Description:** The `acquireWingmanLock` function checks if a lock file is stale via `os.Stat`, then removes it and recreates it. This is a TOCTOU (time-of-check-time-of-use) race. Two processes could both detect a stale lock, both remove it, and both successfully acquire it.
    31→**Suggestion:**
    32→```go
    33→if statErr != nil || time.Since(info.ModTime()) <= staleLockThreshold {
    34→    return false
    35→}
    36→// Use atomic rename instead of remove+create
    37→tempLock := lockPath + ".tmp." + sessionID
    38→if err := os.WriteFile(tempLock, []byte(sessionID), 0o644); err != nil {
    39→    return false
    40→}
    41→if err := os.Rename(tempLock, lockPath); err != nil {
    42→    _ = os.Remove(tempLock)
    43→    return false
    44→}
    45→return true
    46→```
    47→
    48→### [WARNING] Resource leak in spawned detached processes
    49→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman_spawn_unix.go:43-54`
    50→**Description:** When opening the log file fails after the first MkdirAll succeeds, the log file is set to Discard but not closed. Also, if `cmd.Start()` fails after opening the log file, the file is closed, but if it succeeds, the file handle is never closed (only `cmd.Process.Release()` is called).
    51→**Suggestion:**
    52→```go
    53→var logFile *os.File
    54→logDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".entire", "logs")
    55→if mkErr := os.MkdirAll(logDir, 0o750); mkErr == nil {
    56→    if f, openErr := os.OpenFile(filepath.Join(logDir, "wingman.log"),
    57→        os.O_CREATE|os.O_APPEND|os.O_WRONLY, 0o600); openErr == nil {
    58→        logFile = f
    59→        cmd.Stderr = f
    60→        // Close after the detached process starts
    61→        defer func() {
    62→            if logFile != nil {
    63→                _ = logFile.Close()
    64→            }
    65→        }()
    66→    } else {
    67→        cmd.Stderr = io.Discard
    68→    }
    69→}
    70→```
    71→
    72→### [WARNING] Missing error handling in deferred cleanup
    73→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman_review.go:88-92`
    74→**Description:** The deferred lock file removal uses `_ = os.Remove(lockPath)` which silently ignores errors. If removal fails, subsequent reviews will be blocked until manual cleanup or stale threshold expires.
    75→**Suggestion:**
    76→```go
    77→defer func() {
    78→    if err := os.Remove(lockPath); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, os.ErrNotExist) {
    79→        wingmanLog("WARNING: failed to remove lock file: %v", err)
    80→    }
    81→    wingmanLog("lock file removed")
    82→}()
    83→```
    84→
    85→### [WARNING] Unbounded session state iteration
    86→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman_review.go:367-383`
    87→**Description:** The `isSessionIdle` function iterates over all session files without limit. In a repository with many sessions, this could cause performance issues or timeouts.
    88→**Suggestion:**
    89→```go
    90→// Add limit to prevent excessive iteration
    91→const maxSessionCheck = 100
    92→checked := 0
    93→for _, entry := range entries {
    94→    if checked >= maxSessionCheck {
    95→        wingmanLog("hit session check limit (%d), assuming not idle", maxSessionCheck)
    96→        return false
    97→    }
    98→    // ... rest of loop
    99→    checked++
   100→}
   101→```
   102→
   103→### [WARNING] Silent failure modes in triggerWingmanReview
   104→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman.go:193-238`
   105→**Description:** Multiple early-return paths in `triggerWingmanReview` fail silently (e.g., `os.Executable()` error, payload marshal error). These failures leave no trace for debugging why reviews aren't happening.
   106→**Suggestion:**
   107→```go
   108→executable, err := os.Executable()
   109→if err != nil {
   110→    wingmanLog("ERROR: failed to get executable path: %v", err)
   111→    return
   112→}
   113→```
   114→
   115→### [SUGGESTION] Potential denial of service via large diff
   116→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman_prompt.go:132-134`
   117→**Description:** The diff is truncated at 100KB after being fully generated. For very large changes, this wastes time generating a diff that will be discarded. Consider truncating during generation.
   118→**Suggestion:**
   119→```go
   120→// In computeDiff, add a size limit to git diff

   125→### [SUGGESTION] Missing validation for repoRoot parameter
   126→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman.go:84-92` and multiple other locations
   127→**Description:** Functions like `triggerWingmanReview`, `triggerWingmanFromCommit`, etc. accept `repoRoot` but don't validate it's actually a git repository. Malicious or malformed paths could cause unexpected behavior.

   129→```go

   140→### [SUGGESTION] Hardcoded model name
   141→**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/wingman_review.go:38`
   142→**Description:** The review model is hardcoded as "sonnet". Consider making this configurable via settings for flexibility (e.g., using haiku for faster/cheaper reviews on small changes).

> AGENT

Good. Now I have the full picture. Let me address the REVIEW.md.

The review has some valid points. Let me go through them:

1. **[CRITICAL] Command injection in gitDiff** - Disagree. The function is internal, args come from hardcoded constants. Adding shell character validation is security theater for an internal function.

2. **[CRITICAL] Race condition in lock file** - Agree partially. The TOCTOU race is real. However, `os.Rename` is not atomic across different filesystems on all platforms. The current approach with stale threshold is adequate for the use case (best-effort review, not a distributed lock). I'll skip this.

3. **[WARNING] Resource leak in spawned processes** - Agree. The linter already fixed this — the unstaged diffs add `logFile` tracking and close on start failure. These changes are already present.

4. **[WARNING] Missing error handling in deferred lock cleanup** - Agree. Adding a log message is cheap and helpful.

5. **[WARNING] Unbounded session iteration** - Agree. Adding a limit is sensible.

6. **[WARNING] Silent failures in triggerWingmanReview** - Agree. Adding logging for early-return failures is helpful.

7. **[SUGGESTION] Large diff DoS** - Already handled (truncated at 100KB). The cost of generating a slightly larger diff is negligible vs adding complexity.

8. **[SUGGESTION] repoRoot validation** - Disagree. repoRoot comes from `paths.RepoRoot()` which already validates.

9. **[SUGGESTION] Hardcoded model** - Good future idea but out of scope for this change.

Let me implement the changes I agree with.

   190→func triggerWingmanReview(payload WingmanPayload) {
   191→	// Prevent infinite recursion: if we're inside a wingman auto-apply,
   192→	// don't trigger another review. The env var is set by triggerAutoApply.
   193→	if os.Getenv("ENTIRE_WINGMAN_APPLY") != "" {
   194→		return
   195→	}
   196→
   197→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "wingman")
   198→	repoRoot := payload.RepoRoot
   199→
   200→	totalFiles := len(payload.ModifiedFiles) + len(payload.NewFiles) + len(payload.DeletedFiles)
   201→	logging.Info(logCtx, "wingman trigger evaluating",
   202→		slog.String("session_id", payload.SessionID),
   203→		slog.Int("file_count", totalFiles),
   204→	)
   205→
   206→	// Check if a pending REVIEW.md already exists for the current session
   207→	if shouldSkipPendingReview(repoRoot, payload.SessionID) {
   208→		logging.Info(logCtx, "wingman skipped: pending review exists for current session")
   209→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[wingman] Pending review exists, skipping\n")
   210→		return
   211→	}
   212→
   213→	// Atomic lock file prevents concurrent review spawns. O_CREATE|O_EXCL
   214→	// is atomic on all platforms, avoiding the TOCTOU race of Stat+WriteFile.
   215→	lockPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, wingmanLockFile)
   216→	if !acquireWingmanLock(lockPath, payload.SessionID) {
   217→		logging.Info(logCtx, "wingman skipped: review already in progress")
   218→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[wingman] Review in progress, skipping\n")
   219→		return
   220→	}
   221→
   222→	// Dedup check: compute hash of sorted file paths
   223→	allFiles := make([]string, 0, len(payload.ModifiedFiles)+len(payload.NewFiles)+len(payload.DeletedFiles))
   224→	allFiles = append(allFiles, payload.ModifiedFiles...)
   225→	allFiles = append(allFiles, payload.NewFiles...)
   226→	allFiles = append(allFiles, payload.DeletedFiles...)
   227→	filesHash := computeFilesHash(allFiles)
   228→
   229→	state, _ := loadWingmanState() //nolint:errcheck // best-effort dedup
   230→	if state != nil && state.FilesHash == filesHash && state.SessionID == payload.SessionID {
   231→		logging.Info(logCtx, "wingman skipped: dedup hash match",
   232→			slog.String("files_hash", filesHash[:12]),
   233→		)
   234→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[wingman] Already reviewed these changes, skipping\n")
   235→		return
   236→	}
   237→
   238→	// Capture HEAD at trigger time so the detached review diffs against
   239→	// the correct commit even if HEAD moves during the initial delay.
   240→	payload.BaseCommit = resolveHEAD(repoRoot)
   241→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "wingman captured base commit",
   242→		slog.String("base_commit", payload.BaseCommit),
   243→	)

Here's what I addressed from the wingman REVIEW.md:

**Applied:**
1. **Lock file removal logging** — Changed the deferred `os.Remove` in `runWingmanReview` to log a warning on failure (instead of silently ignoring), helping debug cases where lock files block subsequent reviews.
2. **Bounded session iteration** — Added a `maxSessionCheck = 50` limit to `isSessionIdle`'s fallback loop over all session files, preventing performance issues in repos with many sessions.

**Already handled (by linter or existing code):**
3. **Resource leak in spawn** — The unstaged linter changes already add `logFile` tracking and close-on-start-failure for both `spawnDetachedWingmanReview` and `spawnDetachedWingmanApply`.
4. **Timeout on resolveHEAD** — The linter added a 5s context timeout (already in unstaged diffs).

**Skipped (disagreed):**
- Command injection in `gitDiff` — internal function with controlled call sites
- Lock file TOCTOU race — `os.Rename` isn't truly atomic across filesystems; current stale threshold is adequate for best-effort reviews
- repoRoot validation — already validated upstream by `paths.RepoRoot()`
- Large diff optimization — already truncated at 100KB
- Configurable model — good idea but out of scope

I need your permission to run `mise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci` to verify everything compiles and passes. Could you approve those commands?

> AGENT

Based on my investigation, I've identified the root cause: the background `claude --continue --print` auto-apply runs silently and deletes REVIEW.md before the user's next prompt can trigger the visible `additionalContext` injection. The two mechanisms race, and the invisible one wins.

The fix: when there's an active/idle session, **don't** run background auto-apply. Leave REVIEW.md in place so the `additionalContext` injection fires visibly on the user's next prompt. Only use background auto-apply when no session is active.

    19→// wingmanLog writes a timestamped log line to stderr. In the detached subprocess,

    21→func wingmanLog(format string, args ...any) {
